Role of dissipation dilution in achieving high mechanical quality factors in van der Waals resonators

New Submission | Mesoscale and Nanoscale Physics (cond-mat.mes-hall) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Lukas Schleicher, Leonard Geilen, Alexander Musta, Niklas Walter, Anne Rodriguez, Philipp Bredol, Alexander W. Holleitner, Eva M. Weig

Dissipation dilution has driven a revolution in the quality factors ($ Q$ ) of top-down nanomechanical resonators. Yet it has not been leveraged for van der Waals (vdW) materials. Here we report quality factors up to $ 372{,}000\pm37{,}000$ in monolayer MoS$ _2$ drums at 4 K, nearly an order of magnitude beyond the previous best for any vdW material at this temperature, enabled by a clean, polymer-free exfoliation technique. We develop a dissipation-dilution model for circular drums and analyze the contributing intrinsic and extrinsic dissipation mechanisms. The MoS$ _2$ monolayer exhibits a dilution factor of order $ 1,000$ , establishing sub-nanometer-thin resonators as ultimate dilution materials. The intrinsic quality factor, however, is limited by surface losses at atomic thickness. Conversely, multilayer drums show a lower dilution factor but a high intrinsic quality factor, on par with established top-down materials of similar dimensions, both resulting from the increased thickness. A two-level-system analysis of the temperature-dependent damping confirms this bulk-versus-surface picture and provides first insight into its microscopic origin. Our results identify dissipation dilution as the governing mechanism for high-$ Q$ mechanical vdW resonators. Combined with strategies such as soft clamping or stress engineering, they establish atomically thin resonators as a platform for ultrahigh-$ Q$ quantum transduction and sensing.

A Topological Spin on Racetrack Memory

New Submission | Materials Science (cond-mat.mtrl-sci) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Benjamin Zingsem

Magnetic racetrack memory requires localized carriers that combine rapid motion, resistance to perturbations and a detectable signal. Here we show how a 360° domain wall in a hollow ferromagnetic tube combines these properties within a single continuous magnetic shell. Two successive magnetization reversals form a bound object whose surrounding domains point in the same direction. Analytical energetics and micromagnetic simulations establish its finite separation, recovery from tested perturbations and current driven transport without microscopic Dzyaloshinskii-Moriya interaction or a maintaining field. The texture’s winding resists unwinding while radial rotation carries an energy cost, providing a specific control over stability. Relative rotation of the constituent spins accounts quantitatively for additional displacement during current onset. The wall supports motion at hundreds of metres per second, with transient velocities approaching one kilometre per second at stronger drive. A prepared seven-bit pattern retains its carriers and local readout contrast after pulsed transport and recovery. Curved-track and differential-current tests identify radial stiffness and drive uniformity as controls for intact motion. These results establish a physical basis for fast, locally distinguishable tubular magnetic bits and identify a continuous switching pathway that can be targeted by tailored excitation.

Materials Science (cond-mat.mtrl-sci), Mesoscale and Nanoscale Physics (cond-mat.mes-hall), Computational Physics (physics.comp-ph)

Transition-Metal-Induced Uniaxial Anisotropy in YFe$_3$

New Submission | Materials Science (cond-mat.mtrl-sci) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

M. Nur Hasan, Bruno L. Fertig, Santa Pile, Rafael M. Vieira, Alena Vishina, Alexander Kovacs, Gotthard Rieger, Stefan Denneler, Samuel J. R. Holt, Martin Lang, Swapneel Pathak, Andrea Petrocchi, Hans Fangohr, Thomas George Woodcock, Thomas Schrefl, Heike C. Herper

Achieving uniaxial magnetocrystalline anisotropy while preserving high magnetization and thermal stability remains a central challenge in the development of Fe-rich permanent magnets. YFe$ _3$ is a promising candidate owing to its saturation magnetization and Curie temperature, but its intrinsic easy-plane anisotropy limits its potential for permanent-magnet applications. Here, we show that selective transition-metal substitution drives an easy-plane-to-easy-axis transition in YFe$ _3$ . Systematic first-principles screening identifies V, Nb, and Ta as effective dopants, with YFe$ _{2.33}$ Ta$ _{0.67}$ exhibiting a large uniaxial magnetocrystalline anisotropy energy of 2.3MJ/m$ ^{3}$ . Calculated exchange interactions and atomistic Monte Carlo simulations show that the enhanced anisotropy is achieved while preserving robust ferromagnetic order and high thermal stability. Micromagnetic simulations further reveal that YFe$ _{2.33}$ Ta$ _{0.67}$ exhibits the highest coercivity and strongest resistance to irreversible demagnetization, whereas YFe$ _{2.33}$ V$ _{0.67}$ achieves a comparable maximum energy product at 300K. Experimental characterization of V-alloyed YFe$ _3$ shows that the YFe$ _3$ phase can be retained at low nominal V concentrations (1-3 at.%) under the employed synthesis and heat-treatment conditions, whereas higher V contents lead to increasingly multiphase samples. Concentration-dependent MAE calculations reveal a sign reversal between approximately 11 and 14~at.% V, marking the transition from easy-plane to uniaxial easy-axis anisotropy. Consistent with this concentration dependence, electron backscatter diffraction and Kerr microscopy reveal in-plane magnetic domain structures in the experimentally accessible low-V regime. Therefore, our work offers a promising approach to tuning the magnetocrystalline anisotropy of YFe$ _3$ while retaining high Curie temperatures.

Accurately Generalizing Deep-Learning Hamiltonian Methods to Plane-Wave Density Functional Theory

New Submission | Materials Science (cond-mat.mtrl-sci) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Chuntian Wu, Jiaxiang Li, Qiang Xu, Xuecheng Shao, Congwei Xie, Yu Xie, Yanming Ma

Deep neural networks that predict density functional theory (DFT) Hamiltonians from material structures offer a route to efficient electronic-structure calculations. However, extending localized atomic-orbital (AO) Hamiltonian learning to plane-wave (PW) calculations with hybrid functionals requires a compact AO representation that preserves the effects of nonlocal operators. Here we propose a projectability-guided construction of nonorthogonal AO Hamiltonians from a small, selected set of PW eigenstates and eigenvalues. High-projectability states are retained, and selected higher-energy states complete the AO subspace, avoiding an empirical shift and its arbitrary contribution to the learning targets. Because the construction uses PW eigenstates and eigenvalues, it incorporates nonlocal contributions present in the reference calculation without explicitly reconstructing their AO matrix elements. Benchmarks for MoS2 and sodium phases show accurate reproduction of PW bands with smaller AO bases than real-space reconstruction. The resulting PBE and HSE Hamiltonians can be learned by equivariant neural networks, connecting PW reference calculations with electronic-structure prediction. This approach broadens the range of PW DFT calculations available for AO Hamiltonian learning and enables compact, accurate training targets for developing more widely applicable electronic-structure models.

Charge-selective topological locking and ultraslow coarsening in magnetoelastic media

New Submission | Statistical Mechanics (cond-mat.stat-mech) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Tirthankar Banerjee, Amaresh Jaiswal

We demonstrate that the symmetry of magnetoelastic coupling governs the topological organization of composite defects and can qualitatively alter phase-ordering kinetics. In a two-dimensional theory of an in-plane magnetic vector coupled to elastic strain, we find that integer magnetic vortices bind pairs of elementary elastic half-defects. Isotropic magnetostriction produces a core-induced elastic winding independent of the magnetic charge, giving defect binding without charge locking and leaving the magnetic sector with essentially XY-like coarsening. In contrast, quadrupolar magnetoelastic coupling locks magnetic and elastic defect charges: continuous rotational symmetry favors $ q_\phi=q_\theta$ , whereas crystalline anisotropy can reverse the locking to $ q_\phi=-q_\theta$ . We reveal that both locking sectors display anomalously slow vortex annihilation: defect density shows a power-law decay $ t^{-2/5}$ , while the average defect-velocity scales as $ t^{-4/5}$ . These results are consistent with a dynamic exponent $ z\simeq 5$ . We show that this coarsening slowdown is associated with large anisotropic elastic friction. An adiabatic elimination of the elastic field reveals that isotropic magnetostriction merely renormalizes local magnetic interactions, whereas orientational coupling induces effective nonlocal interactions between magnetic spins. Our experimentally testable results thus establish magnetoelastic coupling symmetry as a means of controlling defect topology and ordering kinetics.

Ionic gradients shape the spatial organization and dynamics of supramolecular polymers

New Submission | Soft Condensed Matter (cond-mat.soft) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Claudio Perego, Annalisa Cardellini, Andrea Tagliabue, Giovanni M. Pavan

Living supramolecular materials such as microtubules and actin filaments assemble and function in dynamic, heterogeneous environments, subject to chemical concentration gradients. Their rapid and precise response to such gradients—via aggregation, disassembly, or autonomous motion—underlies the fine spatiotemporal control typical of living systems. Reproducing such responsiveness in synthetic materials is a central challenge in materials science and requires a molecular-level understanding of how gradients couple to supramolecular structure and dynamics, which remains difficult to access experimentally. To this end, we combine implicit-solvent coarse-grained molecular dynamics (MD) with the constant chemical potential MD (C$ {\mu}$ MD) technique to investigate, at molecular resolution, the coupling of two prototypical supramolecular polymer models with externally imposed ionic concentration gradients. Despite their contrasting responses to homogeneous ionic conditions, both systems undergo pronounced spatial reorganization under the gradient, generating regions of locally enhanced supramolecular activity that are inaccessible to homogeneous systems with the same overall content of self-assembling species. Crucially, the local supramolecular dynamics is largely determined by the local concentrations, consistent with the formation of local quasi-equilibria: this direct connection between spatial composition and supramolecular activity emerges as a common governing principle across the two models. These findings shed light on the spatially complex supramolecular response that can be triggered by chemical gradients, and show how a single gradient simulation can encode this response across a continuous range of local chemical environments, thus providing a general strategy to investigate and design materials operating under spatially heterogeneous conditions.

Reversible Magneto-ionic Modification of Metallic Magnetic Thin Films

New Submission | Materials Science (cond-mat.mtrl-sci) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Md Golam Hafiz, Hari Babu Vasili, Philippa Shepley, Mannan Ali, Andrew J. Britton, Oscar Cespedes, Weibin Li, Manuel Valvidares, Rohit Pachat, Wilfried Fotso, Mourad Cherif, Shimpei Ono, Yves Roussigne, Mohamed Belmeguenai, Gavin Burnell

Magnetic multilayers with structural inversion asymmetry, perpendicular magnetic anisotropy (PMA), and large Dzyaloshinskii-Moriya interaction (DMI) are widely studied for magnetic domain wall- and skyrmion-based data processing applications. In this work, we report a controlled, nonvolatile, but reversible local modification of such a system by electrically driven oxygen migration through the metallic film structure by using ionic liquid gating. Our findings provide direct evidence for the modification of the oxidation state at the heavy metal (HM)/ferromagnet (FM) interface. As a result, we observed changes in fundamental magnetic properties, such as an increase in the effective anisotropy constant (Keff) and coercive field (Hc) with oxidation, and a decrease with the reduction of oxygen ions. Positive gate voltages relative to the thin film extract oxygen ions from the magnetic layer, significantly reducing the domain nucleation field. In contrast, negative voltages drive oxygen ions into the magnetic layer, leading to a reversible increase in the extent of domain wall pinning near saturation. We observe a corresponding decrease in the magnetic moment and DMI, with negative voltage reducing both. This magneto-ionic modulation in fully metallic structures is beneficial for spintronic device applications, particularly in field-programmable domain wall and skyrmion devices.

Curie-Weiss cell fluid model: Fitting to experimental critical-point data

New Submission | Soft Condensed Matter (cond-mat.soft) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

I.V. Pylyuk, O.A. Dobush, M.P. Kozlovskii, R.V. Romanik, M.A. Shpot

We examine how the Curie–Weiss cell fluid model can be quantitatively related to real fluids by determining its microscopic parameters from experimental critical-point data for water, carbon dioxide, and three polyol ester lubricants (POE5, POE7, and POE9). The microscopic parameters of the model are determined from the experimental values of the critical temperature and critical density: the global attraction coupling constant is fixed by the critical temperature, and the cell volume by the critical density. The critical pressure then follows as an independent model prediction, deviating from experimental values by 10–41$ %$ across the studied substances. Varying the ratio of local repulsion to global attraction $ f=J_2/J_1$ over a wide range has only a weak effect on this deviation. For $ f\geq1$ , the model’s critical compressibility factor lays within a narrow range $ Z_c\approx0.382-0.386$ , remaining close to the mean-field van der Waals value $ Z_c=3/8=0.375$ and consistently overestimating experimental values. The best agreement with experiment is obtained for the polyol ester lubricants and improves along the POE5–POE7–POE9 series with increasing molar mass, suggesting that multiple-occupancy cell fluid models are well suited to systems of large molecules, particularly soft-matter systems with bounded interaction potentials. We further show that generalizing the model by introducing a temperature- and phase-dependent effective attraction allows it to describe a triple point and to reproduce the broad range of critical-to-triple temperature ratios $ T_c/T_{\mathrm{tr}}$ characteristic of real fluids.

The role of dependency range in thermally coupled superconducting networks

New Submission | Superconductivity (cond-mat.supr-con) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Nahala Yadid, Bnaya Gross, Shlomo Havlin, Nadav M. Shnerb

Interdependent networks can be highly fragile because dependency links amplify local failures into cascades that trigger global collapse. Much of the theoretical understanding comes from geometric network models, where dependency range is imposed externally and coupling strength is distance independent, and it is unclear how these insights apply to real materials, where interdependence arises from physical transport. Here we study thermally coupled amorphous superconducting networks, in which dependencies emerge from current redistribution and heat-mediated interactions governed by thermal diffusivity. Using an experimentally calibrated model of resistively shunted Josephson junctions, we show the system reproduces the three canonical transition regimes: continuous, nucleation-dominated, and mixed-order. However, because heat-driven dependency simultaneously broadens and weakens with distance, long-range thermal coupling can stabilize the superconducting phase upon heating, in contrast with its destabilizing role in geometric models. Cascades are anisotropic, with one-dimensional front propagation. These results offer an experimentally testable perspective complementing the geometric framework.

Altermagnetic Ground States on the Ammann–Beenker Tiling

New Submission | Strongly Correlated Electrons (cond-mat.str-el) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

SeungBeom Hong, YunHo Kim, Moon Jip Park, GiBaik Sim

We identify exact altermagnetic ground states of a classical Heisenberg model on a quasicrystal. Considering the Ammann–Beenker tiling as an example, we determine the ground states by exploiting the parity structure of its parent four-dimensional hypercubic lattice. The ground state hosts coplanar altermagnetic order characterized by the preserved spin-group symmetry $ [C_{2x}\parallel C_{8z}]$ . Without translational symmetry, the momentum-space spin splitting that usually identifies altermagnets is not directly accessible. We instead couple the spins to itinerant electrons and show spin-resolved transport with $ g$ -wave symmetry, confirming the altermagnetic nature in a nonperiodic system. Our results establish a geometric route to altermagnetic order in quasicrystals, connecting exact magnetic ground states to their spin-resolved transport signatures.

Substrate-controlled epitaxial-like growth and photochromic properties of yttrium oxyhydride thin films

New Submission | Materials Science (cond-mat.mtrl-sci) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

M. Zubkins, E. Strods, E. Letko, H. Mändar, D. Moldarev, K. Mizohata, K. Sarakinos, V. Vibornijs, K. Kundzins, J. Purans

Yttrium oxyhydride (YHO) thin films are synthesised by reactive magnetron sputtering of \b{eta}-YHx precursor films onto single-crystalline yttria-stabilised zirconia (YSZ) (111) and (100) substrates at 200 °C, followed by post-deposition oxidation in air. For comparison, reference films are also prepared on amorphous SiO2 and soda-lime glass substrates at both 200 °C and room temperature (RT). The structural (X-ray diffraction and scanning electron microscopy), compositional (elastic recoil detection analysis), and optical properties, including photochromic behaviour, of the films are systematically investigated. The results reveal a strong influence of substrate orientation on the film microstructure and crystallographic texture, leading to epitaxial-like growth with a mosaic spread of 3-4°. YHO deposited on YSZ(100) exhibits larger crystallite sizes (10-20 nm) than YHO grown on YSZ(111) (8 nm) and exhibits residual in-plane compressive strain while remaining largely relaxed with respect to the substrate. Films deposited at 200 °C exhibit lower oxygen concentrations (O/Y = 0.71-0.82) than films deposited at RT (O/Y = 0.92-1.26). All films exhibit photochromic behaviour under solar simulator illumination, with relative transmittance changes ranging from 7 to 22 % after 10 min of illumination. Despite the pronounced structural differences, the epitaxial-like films show no systematic improvement in photochromic response, indicating that the effect is governed primarily by composition and the local O/H configuration rather than by long-range cation ordering. These findings provide new insights into the relationship between crystallographic texture, composition, and photochromic properties in YHO thin films, contributing to the understanding of this material for optical and optoelectronic applications.

Quantitative Modelling of Solvation in 2D Materials via Alchemical Decoupling and Potential Energy Surfaces

New Submission | Materials Science (cond-mat.mtrl-sci) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Lindsey N. Ioos, Duncan A. Peterson, Scott C. Warren

Understanding why 2D materials remain suspended in solutions is essential to understanding the mechanisms of aggregation and exfoliation. These dynamics have been difficult to model because of the long timescales and large activation energies involved in these simulations. Here we describe an improved strategy for modelling the solvation and stability of 2D materials that uses the Gibbs free energy of solvation to accurately calibrate two disconnected potential energy surfaces for the fully desolvated and fully solvated 2D materials. By joining these surfaces using solvation energy, we obtain predictions for 2D material stability in various solvents that are qualitatively different than prior simulations and that have excellent agreement with experimental results. Our approach predicts a much higher barrier to full desolvation of the material, suggesting that materials that appear to be aggregated in fact still retain monolayers of solvent between adjacent layers of 2D materials. This work provides much needed understanding on the interactions between the solvent and 2D material that can guide future experimental work.

Materials Science (cond-mat.mtrl-sci), Mesoscale and Nanoscale Physics (cond-mat.mes-hall), Soft Condensed Matter (cond-mat.soft)

Macroscopic fluctuation theory of stochastic systems undergoing monitoring

New Submission | Statistical Mechanics (cond-mat.stat-mech) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

João Costa, Pedro Ribeiro, Andrea De Luca

We develop a macroscopic fluctuation theory for diffusive classical lattice gases subject to continuous coarse-grained monitoring. Treating measurements in the typical-outcome (quenched) regime, we show that observation adds a quadratic confining term to the MFT action that pins the density to its hydrodynamic profile. We derive sufficient conditions under which, in the presence of monitoring, the current large-deviation function remains convex, the additivity principle holds, and dynamical phase transitions are excluded. For particle-hole-symmetric one-dimensional models we find that monitoring suppresses dynamical phase transitions and can change their order from second to first, as we demonstrate in the Katz–Lebowitz–Spohn model. In the strong-measurement limit, we find that the current distribution becomes Gaussian and determine the scaling of its cumulants: they follow simple power laws when the mobility remains nonzero throughout the system, but exhibit anomalous logarithmic scaling when the mobility vanishes at the boundaries.

Flexoelectricity and Flexomagnetism

New Submission | Materials Science (cond-mat.mtrl-sci) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Alexander Edström, Massimiliano Stengel

Strain gradients, unlike homogeneous strain, are odd under space inversion and unlock couplings that are strictly forbidden in the parent crystal. At the nanoscale, and in curved low-dimensional systems in particular, such gradients are readily attainable and often unavoidable, so that curvature acts as a symmetry-breaking field written into the lattice itself. This chapter reviews the resulting couplings to electric polarization and magnetism. After establishing the geometric relation between strain gradients and curvature, we review flexoelectricity as the better-established reference case: the bulk, surface and dynamic contributions that complicate its definition, its microscopic mechanisms, its manifestation in two-dimensional materials, and the first-principles methodology now available for its calculation. Using the magnetoelectric effect as a conceptual bridge, we then develop flexomagnetism in a broad sense, covering any coupling phenomena between magnetism and strain gradients, and specifically discuss the linear magnetization response to curvature and curvature-induced Dzyaloshinskii-Moriya interactions, beyond the established curvilinear treatment. Finally, we briefly describe some additional effects fitting under this broad umbrella of flexomagnetism, including nonlinear ones, inhomogeneous magnetoelectric and Rashba-type phenomena, and connections to altermagnetism.

Materials Science (cond-mat.mtrl-sci)

Phases of a rotating Bose-Einstein condensate trapped in a hard-wall potential

New Submission | Quantum Gases (cond-mat.quant-gas) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Hélène Perrin, G. M. Kavoulakis

We investigate the rotational properties of a zero-temperature Bose-Einstein condensed gas, which is confined in a hard-wall potential. For weak interactions the gas carries its angular momentum via vortices of multiple quantization. As the coupling increases and for sufficiently slow rotational frequencies of the trap, the multiply-quantized vortex states become unstable against a mixed state. For stronger couplings, as the angular frequency of the trap increases, there is a transition from a vortex lattice, to a vortex lattice with a hole at the trap center, and finally to a giant vortex. For weak effective attractive interactions we still have vortices of multiple quantization. These states become unstable towards states which break the axial symmetry of the problem and resemble center-of-mass excitation, as the coupling becomes more negative. Finally, for even more attractive forces, the system becomes unstable against collapse.

Imaging Gate-Tunable Single-Layer Molecular Kagome Lattices

New Submission | Materials Science (cond-mat.mtrl-sci) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Ziyi Wang, Rafal Zuzak, Weichen Tang, Haowen Pang, Yutang Qiao, Dhanvanth Balakrishnan, Yuqiao Yan, Aining Hu, Andrew Sun, Takashi Taniguchi, Kenji Watanabe, Feng Wang, Steven G. Louie, Michael F. Crommie

Kagome lattices provide a simple geometry for engineering electronic states that exhibit novel quantum behavior. In conventional kagome materials, however, the relevant states are often embedded in chemically complex three-dimensional crystals and are difficult to isolate or tune. Molecule-based kagome networks offer structural precision and tunability, but have largely been confined to metallic substrates incompatible with 2D devices. Here we report growth of single-layer Ni$ _3$ (HITP)$ _2$ metal-organic frameworks (MOFs) on van der Waals substrates including graphene-based field-effect devices using metal-salt molecular beam epitaxy. Low-temperature scanning tunneling spectroscopy resolves kagome-derived flat-band and van Hove singularity-based spectral features consistent with first-principles calculations. Gating hybrid 2D MOF devices allows us to energetically shift kagome spectral features, thus establishing electrostatic control of chemically programmable 2D kagome electronic lattices.

A Transferable Machine-Learned Potential Enabling Large-Scale Copper-Carbon Simulations from Equilibrium to Extreme Conditions

New Submission | Materials Science (cond-mat.mtrl-sci) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Feiyu Zhou, Wei Qiu, Yan Chen, Jiahui Liu, Yilun Liu, Shengying Yue, Penghua Ying

Cu-C materials and interfaces are central to diamond thermal management, metallization, graphene growth and carbon-coating technologies, but atomistic simulations across these applications require a potential that describes metallic Cu, diverse carbon bonding, heterogeneous Cu-C interactions and close atomic encounters. Here we develop NEP-CuC, a transferable neuroevolution potential trained on a dataset of 2,727 uniformly relabelled configurations selected from a heterogeneous Cu-C candidate pool. Disagreement within an ensemble of five independently trained models provides an internal indicator of model sensitivity. A Ziegler-Biersack-Littmark (ZBL) correction describes short-range nuclear repulsion, while a runtime D3(BJ) correction supplements dispersion-sensitive calculations. Tests on source-derived held-out and independent external datasets, together with structural, elastic, defect, interfacial, vibrational and finite-temperature transport benchmarks, establish broad but property-dependent physical fidelity across elemental Cu, diverse carbon environments and heterogeneous Cu-C configurations. Large-scale simulations of amorphous-carbon formation and orientation-dependent shock compression of Cu further test transferability under strongly non-equilibrium conditions. For Cu/diamond interfaces, the same potential describes deposition, annealing and tensile response: preparation history leaves persistent differences in interphase structure, while the mean peak nominal tensile stress at 500 K decreases from $ 12.35\pm0.71$ to $ 6.65\pm0.98$ GPa as the incident Cu energy during the initial deposition stage increases from 1 to 50 eV. NEP-CuC provides an atomistic framework for linking processing, interfacial structure and physical response across Cu-C systems, with direct relevance to Cu/diamond thermal-management interfaces, diamond metallization and non-equilibrium Cu-C processing.

Direct growth of crystalline spinel GaON films: A wide-bandgap mixed-anion semiconductor

New Submission | Materials Science (cond-mat.mtrl-sci) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Baikui Li, Zecheng Yang, Wenhao Yin, Zijing Wu, Haitao Zhao, Yunwei Sheng, Honglei Wu, Jiannong Wang

Mixed-anion semiconductors offer opportunities for bandgap engineering beyond conventional multication alloys, but competing phases and disorder complicate their synthesis and the identification of their electronic properties. Gallium oxynitride (GaON) could connect the technologically important GaN and Ga2O3 systems, yet establishing a clear relation between its crystal structure, composition and bandgap remains challenging. Here we report the direct growth of crystalline spinel gamma-GaON films by nitrogen-plasma-assisted pulsed-laser deposition. Varying the nitrogen and oxygen supply yields monoclinic beta-Ga2O3, predominantly disordered films and crystalline spinel GaON. Complementary diffraction, atomic-resolution microscopy and elemental analysis establish the spinel structure and nitrogen incorporation. Temperature-dependent photocurrent spectroscopy reveals a clear redshift of the crystalline gamma-GaON response, supporting assignment of a band-edge transition near 4.3 eV at room temperature. These results open opportunities to investigate mixed-anion wide-bandgap semiconductor physics and develop heterostructures.

Time-Resolved Phonon Dispersions of Thermally Excited Graphite at the Nanoscale

New Submission | Materials Science (cond-mat.mtrl-sci) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Mairi McCauley, Christoph T. Koch, Benedikt Haas (Department of Physics and Center for the Science of Materials Berlin, Humboldt-Universität zu Berlin, Berlin, Germany)

Lattice vibrations govern the propagation of heat in materials, and investigating their dynamics through time-resolved phonon dispersion measurements would provide insight into these excitations and their interactions. However, time-resolved measurements that directly resolve the phonon dispersion at the nanoscale have not been previously demonstrated. Momentum-resolved vibrational electron energy loss spectroscopy (EELS) provides access to phonon energies and momenta with nanoscale spatial resolution. Here, we combine momentum-resolved vibrational EELS with pulsed laser heating and synchronised gated electron detection to observe the phonon dispersion of graphite during controlled heating and cooling on the microsecond timescale. Recording both the gain and loss spectra at defined times relative to laser excitation, we determine the temperature at each time delay and follow changes to the phonon branches as a function of temperature. The momentum-resolved spectra reveal a decrease in energy of the optical phonon branch with increasing temperature accompanied by an increase in peak width. These changes are consistent with temperature-dependent phonon renormalisation and scattering associated with anharmonic interactions. The approach is compatible with detector gating on timescales down to 50 ns, providing a route towards extending these measurements to faster dynamics. This work demonstrates time-resolved phonon dispersion spectroscopy using vibrational EELS and provides a route towards probing temperature-dependent phonon dynamics at the nanoscale with simultaneous energy and momentum resolution.

Materials Science (cond-mat.mtrl-sci)

Exact Microscopic One-Body Reduction in the Symmetric Exclusion Process

New Submission | Statistical Mechanics (cond-mat.stat-mech) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Olivier Bénichou, Aurélien Grabsch

The symmetric exclusion process (SEP) is a paradigmatic model of interacting particle transport. Its current–density correlations are governed by an infinite many-body hierarchy of equations. Only recently, this hierarchy was found to collapse at large scales onto a single closed equation. Is this remarkable simplification created by coarse graining, or does it reflect a deeper property already present in the microscopic dynamics? Here we show that the second scenario is realized. At arbitrary time on the lattice, the entire hierarchy collapses onto a single linear equation which is exactly equivalent to a persistence problem for a single auxiliary random walker. Thus an interacting many-body problem reduces exactly to a genuine one-body problem before any scaling limit is taken. This reduction determines the complete finite-time correlation profile, including during nonequilibrium relaxation. More broadly, the microscopic operator uncovered here opens a route to more general correlation functions in the SEP, and may point to a general organizing principle for nonequilibrium interacting particle systems.

Plasmon modes in triple-point semimetal, its quadratic cousin, and Hopf semimetal

New Submission | Mesoscale and Nanoscale Physics (cond-mat.mes-hall) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Ipsita Mandal

We study collective charge excitations in pseudospin-1 triple-point semimetals within the random-phase approximation, considering both the linearised model with a flat-band and its quadratic-corrected counterpart. We analyse their density response, Drude weights, screening, plasmon dispersion and damping, particle-hole continua, and Friedel oscillations from the long-wavelength to the arbitrary-momentum regime. The quadratic correction renders the flat-band dispersive and endows it with a Fermi surface, thereby affecting the overall response. Comparisons with Weyl and Hopf semimetals reveal how bandstructure and quantum geometry jointly govern collective charge dynamics. While the Drude weights are determined by the Fermi surface and band-velocities, interband screening, plasmon dispersion, and Friedel oscillations reflect the quantum geometry of the eigenstates.

Momentum conservation for ab initio calculations with pseudopotentials via a phase space electronic structure formulation

New Submission | Other Condensed Matter (cond-mat.other) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Guy Ohad, Nadine C. Bradbury, Joseph E. Subotnik

We demonstrate how to build a robust phase space electronic Hamiltonian $ \hat{H}_\text{PS}(\boldsymbol{R},\boldsymbol{P})$ that depends on nuclear position $ \boldsymbol{R}$ and nuclear momentum $ \boldsymbol{P}$ in the presence of ionic pseudopotentials. In particular, we show that in order to account for the non-local component of a pseudopotential while also conserving linear and angular momentum, one is forced to introduce a non-local one-electron operator $ \hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}^\text{nl}$ that couples electronic and nuclear momenta together. To test the validity of our ansatz, we derive a generalized form of Nafie’s exact equality (which relates electronic momentum and electronic position) so as to include the presence of pseudopotentials. We then benchmark our proposed phase space electronic structure theory against this generalized Nafie-like result for a diverse set of diatomic molecules spanning a wide range of elements. We find excellent agreement across this ab initio test set, highlighting the strength of our method in capturing meaningful electronic momentum beyond the Born-Oppenheimer framework.

Other Condensed Matter (cond-mat.other)

Extending the mean first-passage-time formalism to the reconstruction of high free-energy barriers: A top-start approach

New Submission | Statistical Mechanics (cond-mat.stat-mech) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Ying Jiang, Ivan Saika-Voivod

Rare events, such as crystal nucleation in mildly supercooled liquids, are characterized by waiting times that can be much longer than the timescale of the underlying microscopic dynamics. The mean first-passage time (MFPT) formalism [J. Wedekind and D. Reguera, J. Phys. Chem. B 112, 11060 (2008)] provides a route to reconstruct one-dimensional free-energy profiles from the MFPT and steady-state distribution measured along a reaction coordinate, but conventional implementations become inefficient for sufficiently high barriers because spontaneous crossings are rarely observed. We introduce a “top-start” extension that initiates trajectories near the barrier top and reconstructs the free-energy profile from their subsequent evolution away from the barrier toward lower-free-energy regions. We establish the equivalence between the top-start and two-region constructions using a Markov-chain transition-matrix formulation. We validate the method using one-dimensional random-walk simulations across low, medium, and high free-energy barriers, with transition-matrix calculations providing an exact benchmark for the corresponding discretized models. The top-start reconstruction remains accurate in the high-barrier regime where conventional MFPT sampling becomes prohibitively inefficient.

Dark state preparation in a zero-field-split molecular triplet

New Submission | Mesoscale and Nanoscale Physics (cond-mat.mes-hall) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Maria Pfahler, Andrea Donarini

We propose a protocol to prepare and probe dark states in a single-molecule electron spin resonance based on atomic force microscopy (ESR-AFM), thus extending existing techniques developed to address Rabi oscillations. To this end, we simulate in analogy to atomic physics the coherent manipulation of a three-level system in the V-configuration using a multifrequency magnetic field. More specifically, we consider a neutral molecule with a zero-field-split (ZFS) first excited triplet and analyze within a pump-probe scheme the dynamics of the total triplet population. In the triplet subspace, the dark state is a coherent superposition formed by two ZFS states which does not evolve in time despite a continuous resonant driving towards the third one. We characterize the properties of this state comparing it to its fully incoherent counterpart. Within a density-matrix formalism for open systems, we then compute the expected dark state signatures both for ideal scenarios and for the realistic parameterization of ESR-AFM on pentacene.

Composition-controlled optical fingerprints and anisotropy of self-trapped excitons in double perovskites

New Submission | Materials Science (cond-mat.mtrl-sci) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Simone Grillo, Offir Zachs Maishlos, Liya Koren, Rotem Strassberg, Rachel Lifer, Edi Maizenberg, Umberto De Giovannini, Angel Rubio, Yehonadav Bekenstein, Ofer Neufeld

Double perovskites (DPs) offer a compositionally versatile platform for photocatalysis and optoelectronics, but their soft lattices favor strong exciton-phonon coupling and self-trapped excitons (STEs), which produce broad, red-shifted photoluminescence (PL). Here, we combine optical spectroscopy and ab initio calculations to study lead-free, Sb-doped Cs2BInCl6 nanocrystals with B-site alloying from Na to K. Alloying tunes the PL energy from about 2.48 to 2.70 eV, while absorption features near 3.8 eV remain nearly unchanged. Calculations reveal that Sb doping enables bright radiative recombination channels and delocalizes the excitonic wavefunction. We identify STEs responsible for the large PL red shift, associated with an upward shift of dopant-hybridized in-gap states. Na-to-K substitution selectively changes the STE lattice distortion, explaining why PL varies strongly with composition while absorption does not. Theory further predicts pronounced PL polarization anisotropy that reflects the STE wavefunction’s spatial structure and depends on B-site composition. Time-resolved anisotropy measurements closely agree with these predictions, providing experimental fingerprints of the excitonic wavefunction and its evolution with alloying. Together, these results disentangle the roles of doping and alloying in lead-free DPs and establish polarization-resolved spectroscopy as a probe of STE wavefunctions.

Superexchange coupling between a dilute Gd3+ probe and orphan spins in a valence-bond glass

New Submission | Strongly Correlated Electrons (cond-mat.str-el) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Patrick Colgan, Artur Solodovnyk, Jeremy P. Carlo, Scott Dietrich

The valence-bond-glass state of the 4d^1 double perovskite Ba2YMoO6 combines Mo^5+ singlets with residual paramagnetic “orphan” spins whose coupling is hard to reach with bulk probes. We probe them by electron paramagnetic resonance of a dilute Gd^3+ ion on the Y^3+ site via broadband coplanar-waveguide transmission at 5 K. The isostructural nonmagnetic niobate Ba2YNbO6, doped with the same Gd^3+ probe, is a fixed-lattice control. The Gd^3+ linewidth nearly doubles in the magnetic host. Dipolar coupling to the sparse orphans is nearly an order of magnitude too weak; the excess is instead reproduced by a transferred f-d superexchange of J ~ 0.2 K, so the width is a static measure of orphan-spin coupling. The half-field (Delta m_S = +/-2) satellite collapses in the magnetic host, a dynamical dephasing channel, while the unchanged g-factor marks a zero-mean random internal field. Together the two channels probe the coupling and dynamics of valence-bond-glass orphan spins.

Emergent Loop Geometry in Gauge-Equivariant Graph Neural Networks

New Submission | Statistical Mechanics (cond-mat.stat-mech) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Ali Rayat, Gia-Wei Chern

Gauge-equivariant graph neural networks generate ordered products of gauge links through local message passing, but the geometry of these internal structures is not captured by the receptive field alone. We investigate the statistical ensemble of closed lattice walks generated by recursive node- and edge-feature operations, independently of network training. Analytical and numerical results reveal a separation between rapid growth of contour length and much slower spatial expansion: deeper compositions generate increasingly long paths through repeated traversal of relatively compact regions. Loop expansions of fermionic observables provide a physical interpretation of this hierarchy, showing how spatial locality remains compatible with high-order dependence on nearby gauge fields. These findings identify complementary roles of network depth in extending spatial reach and enriching the representations constructed within that reach, providing a geometrical perspective on learning lattice gauge observables.

16 pages, 8 figures

Competing delocalisation and localisation induced by the dipolar tail in fragmented lattice gases

New Submission | Quantum Gases (cond-mat.quant-gas) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Sabhyata Gupta, Adith Sai Aramthottil, Luis Santos

We investigate how the long-range tail of dipole-dipole interactions modifies the dynamics of strongly interacting hard-core bosons in a one-dimensional lattice. In the nearest-neighbour model, large interactions generate an emergent Hilbert-space fragmentation into manifolds labelled by the number of nearest-neighbour bonds (N_{\mathrm{NN}}). The dipolar tail has a non-monotonic effect on this fragmented dynamics as the interaction-to-tunnelling ratio (V/t) is increased. At intermediate (V/t), it enhances delocalisation relative to the nearest-neighbour model by reducing the energetic detuning between neighbouring (N_{\mathrm{NN}}) manifolds and sustaining their hybridisation by hopping. At larger (V/t), the same tail lifts degeneracies within each manifold, beginning with the splitting associated with the next-nearest-neighbour bond number (N_{\mathrm{NNN}}), and promotes intra-manifold localisation and Hilbert-space shattering. The competition between these two effects produces a crossover from a delocalising to a localising role of the dipolar tail, visible in the late-time spatial inhomogeneity. This crossover occurs for nearly all initial states considered and persists at different fillings. Transverse confinement strengthens the short-range part of the dipolar tail and shifts the crossover to lower (V/t), bringing it into the regime relevant to current experiments with magnetic atoms and polar molecules.

Spin textures at the Fermi surface of ferromagnets and associated torque

New Submission | Mesoscale and Nanoscale Physics (cond-mat.mes-hall) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

T. Valet, J. Li, H. Jaffrès, V. Cros, M. Chshiev, R. Raimondi

We establish theoretically that transverse spin textures are generically induced by the spin-orbit interaction at the Fermi surface of centrosymmetric ferromagnetic metals. We confirm this prediction with {\em ab initio} computations for Fe, Co and Ni. By symmetry, this cannot be associated with spin-orbit torques under a uniform current. However, a new {\em current-gradient-induced} torque emerges in nanoscale geometries. For a series of Ni-based bilayers, the computed Ni thickness dependence of the current-gradient-induced torque is in quantitative agreement with measurements previously attributed to orbital torques. We discuss how this opens a new avenue for the electrical control of magnetic orders, possibly also including the Néel vector of metallic altermagnets.

Towards synergy between theory and experiments: First-passage processes under resetting with stochastic return protocols

New Submission | Statistical Mechanics (cond-mat.stat-mech) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Ashutosh Dubey, Arnab Pal

Stochastic resetting with stochastic return (SR) has recently emerged as an effective and practically relevant strategy for controlling first-passage phenomena and optimizing search processes by significantly reducing completion times in a wide class of stochastic systems. While most theoretical studies assume instantaneous return (IR), in realistic systems the return to the resetting position typically occurs over a finite time. We consider a 1-d diffusing particle in a harmonic trap with stiffnesses $ k_1$ and $ k_2$ during search and return, respectively. The particle searches for the target and, upon resetting at rate $ r$ , enters the return phase, continuing until it reaches either the origin or the absorbing target at $ L>0$ . Notably, the model permits target detection throughout the return phase, distinguishing it from IR scenarios. We analytically derive the MFPT for our model and determine parameter regions where SR enhances or suppresses search efficiency over the underlying dynamics, constructing the corresponding phase diagram using the coefficient of variation criterion. We further identify regimes in which SR is more efficient than IR using the SR criterion. This behavior is tested across different parameter sets taken from experimental studies by Goerlich et al., Biroli et al., and Besga et al. We map the parameter values reported in existing experimental studies onto our theoretical phase diagram, thereby identifying the regimes in which SR outperforms IR. In particular, we find that the most relevant region of the phase space, where SR is most beneficial corresponds to larger values of the potential strength in the search phase and lower values of potential in the return phase. Our results provide experimentally testable predictions and bridge the gap between theoretical resetting models and optical trap mediated finite-time return dynamics.

Statistical Mechanics (cond-mat.stat-mech)

Single anyons and anyon binding in a quantum Hall dot

New Submission | Mesoscale and Nanoscale Physics (cond-mat.mes-hall) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Marco Valentini, Will Wang, Amir Shapour Mohammadi, Noah Samuelson, Chengxuan Li, Liam Cohen, Mark Zakharyan, Jiechao Feng, Taige Wang, Takashi Taniguchi, Kenji Watanabe, Michael P. Zaletel, Andrea F. Young

Anyons are particles with quantized fractional statistics. In theory, a single anyon can be stabilized as a localized fractional charge within the bulk of a topologically ordered two-dimensional insulator. However, experiments to date have focused on probing anyons in structures in which many anyonic excitations coexist and interact. Here, we demonstrate the trapping of a single anyon in a device consisting of a graphene-based quantum point contact with an embedded tunable quantum Hall dot. Benchmarking our device at v = 1, we demonstrate backscattering between Fermi liquid edge states mediated by the whole-electron Coulomb blockade of the dot. The cleanliness and control available in our device allow depletion of the dot to the last quasihole, and we observe spin blockade for transitions to the opposite-spin Landau level. At v = 1/3 and 2/3, we observe backscattering resonances with three times the frequency, consistent with resonant transmission of e/3 charges via a single anyon transistor. We demonstrate controlled depletion to the last quasiparticle to form a single anyon quantum dot. Tracing the charge addition spectrum for quasiparticle number Nqp = 1-5, we observe highly irregular addition energies, consistent with DMRG modeling showing a complex orbital shell-filling behavior. At higher Nqp = 10-20, the addition energies show a striking pattern: quasiholes at 1/3 (and quasiparticles at 2/3) show a regular addition spectrum while quasiparticles at 1/3 (and quasiholes at 2/3) do not. We trace the origin of this irregularity to an attractive component of the inter-anyon interaction: by experimentally tuning the confining energy, we observe complete suppression of alternating charge sectors, after which the dot charges in increments of twice the fundamental charge. This observation indicates the emergence of an anyon binding energy as large as the charging energy.

Mesoscale and Nanoscale Physics (cond-mat.mes-hall)

A hierarchy of exact lower bounds on the Laughlin spectral gap

New Submission | Strongly Correlated Electrons (cond-mat.str-el) | 2026-10-14 20:00 EDT

Michael G. Scheer, Eslam Khalaf

We develop a bootstrap approach to deriving lower bounds on the spectral gap of the first Haldane pseudopotential Hamiltonian on the sphere with $ N_\Phi$ flux quanta for arbitrary $ N_\Phi$ , including in the thermodynamic limit $ N_\Phi \to \infty$ . Our approach can in principle be generalized to pseudopotentials describing other model wavefunctions such as the Moore-Read state or Read-Rezayi sequence. Our approach is based on Knabe’s observation that if $ H$ is a Hamiltonian with ground state energy $ 0$ then the spectral gap is at least $ \epsilon > 0$ if and only if $ H^2 \succeq \epsilon H$ . To show this inequality, we split $ H^2$ into two parts, one that can be analytically bounded and another that can be bounded using a finite semidefinite program (SDP). Crucially, the size of this program is determined only by cutoff parameters, and the program depends on $ N_\Phi$ only parametrically, allowing us to obtain numerically exact (up to solver precision) lower bounds on the gap. In order to turn these numerical bounds into mathematically rigorous bounds, we introduce an algorithm that can produce nearly-optimal exact feasible points for large rational SDPs. We additionally provide an algorithm that establishes the rank stability of the semidefinite program in the limit $ N_\Phi \to \infty$ which is needed to certify the bound in the thermodynamic limit. Beyond establishing the Laughlin gap and revealing a potential method for deriving spectral gap lower bounds for other pseudopotentials, our work provides a general method for turning numerical semidefinite programming bounds into rigorous mathematical proofs, which may have wide applicability.

Research Square

Frequency-agile integrated mid-infrared electro-optic comb

Article | Nonlinear optics | 2026-10-13 20:00 EDT

Pierre Didier, Prakhar Jain, Tristan Kuttner, Oliver Pitz, Rachel Grange

Mid-infrared frequency combs provide highly sensitive and selective access to molecular vibrational transitions, making them attractive for integrated spectroscopic sensing. However, compact sources offering broad wavelength coverage together with independent electronic control of comb parameters remain challenging to realize. Here, we demonstrate mid-infrared electro-optic frequency-comb generation through difference-frequency generation in periodically poled thin-film lithium niobate waveguides implemented on two substrates, namely silicon dioxide and sapphire. Our architecture combines a continuous-wave near-infrared pump with a telecom-band electro-optic comb operated in either single- or dual-comb configurations, generated on-chip. Difference-frequency generation allows downconversion of the telecom comb to the mid-infrared. The comb spacing is defined by the drive radio frequency, while the comb center frequency is independently controlled by the telecom seed wavelength. The quasi-phase-matching condition can be further tuned through temperature control. We generate mid-infrared combs with an instantaneous bandwidth of approximately 6 nm and a center-wavelength tuning range exceeding 200 nm on a single device. A direct comparison of the device on two substrates reveals the advantage of lithium niobate on sapphire for operation beyond 3.5 µm, providing higher output power, improved conversion efficiency, and extended spectral coverage up to 4.5 µm. Finally, we demonstrate on-chip dual-comb generation in the mid-infrared with a tunable relative frequency offset between the two combs. Low-frequency beat notes between corresponding comb lines are detected over a 140 nm MIR wavelength range. This unique integrated mid-infrared electro-optic comb platform combines independent electronic control of the comb center frequency, line spacing, and frequency offset with on-chip dual-comb generation.

Vapor-liquid-solid epitaxy of wafer-scale perovskite single crystals

Article | Surfaces, interfaces and thin films | 2026-10-13 20:00 EDT

Guifu Zou, Yuan Chen, Guijia Hu, Jianmei Chen, Wei Zhang, Yongshu Wang, Hongcao Shi, Jinchuan Cao, Guoxiang Zhao, Chen Wang, Peipei Ma, Hao Wang, Shan Cong

Wafer-scale halide perovskite single-crystal films are highly sought after for integrated optoelectronic applications. However, conventional vapor-phase growth suffers from poor substrate wettability and restricted precursor diffusion, consequences of the high interfacial energies inherent to vapor-solid interfaces at both the vapor-substrate and vapor-film boundaries. Here, we report catalyst-free vapor-liquid-solid (VLS) growth of 2-inch perovskite single-crystal wafers. This process is mediated by a condensed halide-rich liquid intermediate that establishes complete surface wetting and replaces high-energy vapor-solid interfaces with low-energy liquid-solid interfaces, thereby substantially reducing the nucleation energy barrier and nucleation density. The liquid-phase medium also dramatically extends metal halide solute migration distances, enabling edge-dominated layer growth and seamless coalescence of oriented nuclei. We demonstrate the generality of this approach by fabricating wafer-scale films of CsPbBr₃, CsPbCl₃, and their alloys. Atomic-scale microscopy and wafer-scale spectroscopy confirm exceptional crystalline uniformity, ultralow trap-state density, and superior electronic and optoelectronic quality.
